The tempered disk and the tempered cohomology
F. Bambozzi, B. Chiarellotto, P. Vanni

Abstract
Let 𝒱 \mathcal{\mathchar 29014} be a complete discretely valued ring of mixed characteristic, with fraction field K \mathchar 29003 and residue field k \mathchar 29035 . Using the ind-Banach framework for derived analytic geometry, we view generic fibers of 𝒱 \mathcal{\mathchar 29014} -schemes as derived analytic spaces. This approach yields a refined notion of spectrum, richer than that of classical rigid geometry: for example we may have open subsets whose structure sheaf contains functions of logarithmic growth. In this setting, the transfer theorem for the log-growth of solutions of p \mathchar 29040 -adic differential equations becomes a natural continuity statement, analogous to the classical transfer theorem for radii of convergence on Berkovich spaces. Motivated by ideas of Scholze, we introduce tempered tubular neighborhoods of smooth k \mathchar 29035 -schemes and define a new tempered de Rham cohomology via the Hodge-completed derived de Rham complex of these tubes. Finally, we establish a comparison theorem showing that, for smooth proper k \mathchar 29035 -schemes, tempered de Rham cohomology agrees with crystalline cohomology.



1
Introduction

In the classical theory of
\mathchar 29040
-adic differential equations, the presence of a Frobenius structure imposes strong constraints on the radii of convergence of solutions. This phenomenon is often referred to as Dwork’s trick
[
27
, Lemma 6.3]
,
[
29
]
. Such Frobenius structures typically arise from geometry, for instance from families of varieties, and the corresponding good convergence properties can be expressed either by saying that the differential equation is solvable at the generic point or, in terms of coefficients, by saying that it is overconvergent. Moreover, there is a finer relation between the Frobenius structure and the growth of solutions: as observed by Dwork
[
21
]
, the slopes of Frobenius are related to the logarithmic growth of
solutions.

Berkovich and Huber spaces provide a natural framework in which information can be transferred from generic points to classical points in rigid analytic geometry. In these theories both types of points belong to a single topological space associated with the analytic space. This makes it possible to transfer information, such as radii of convergence, by using continuity properties of the radius function
[
2
]
,
[
44
]
. In this picture, radius conditions are represented geometrically by analytic domains. By contrast, logarithmic growth conditions are not represented by open analytic domains in the usual rigid or Berkovich geometry in the same direct way. Nevertheless, classical transfer theorems for logarithmic growth are available
[
24
]
,
[
21
]
,
[
22
]
.

In this article we work in derived analytic geometry setting provided ind-Banach algebras, and use it to associate to such algebras a spectrum. In this spectrum there are open subsets whose sections of the structure sheaf are functions with logarithmic growth. This allows us to reinterpret transfer theorems for logarithmic growth as continuity statements. In this sense, the framework developed here should be viewed as a natural analytic setting for studying the continuity of logarithmic growth conditions, in analogy with the study of radii of convergence in
[
44
]
, and for studying
\mathchar 28998
-isocrystals with logarithmic decay in the sense of
[
36
]
,
[
37
]
.

From this perspective, and inspired by ideas introduced by Scholze
[
47
]
, the usual radius of convergence condition for power series is replaced by a logarithmic growth condition. In dimension one this leads to the tempered open disk, which is an open subset of our spectrum containing the open unit disk and contained in the closed unit disk. This suggests a tempered analogue of convergent rigid cohomology (where the open disk is used).

Recall that convergent rigid cohomology of a variety in characteristic
\mathchar 29040
is computed by considering tubes of radius one inside the generic fibers of smooth formal
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
-schemes into which the variety is embedded. The independence of the resulting cohomology from the auxiliary choices is a fundamental property of the theory. In the convergent setting, coefficients are convergent isocrystals, and the convergence condition may be viewed, in dimension one, as a condition that the generic radius of convergence is equal to one. It is therefore natural to replace the classical tube by a tempered tube and to define a corresponding tempered cohomology theory for schemes over a field
\mathchar 29035
of characteristic
\mathchar 29040
.

For this new theory, one starts with a closed regular embedding

\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29035}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\widehat{\mathchar 29008}

of a
\mathchar 29035
-scheme into the special fiber of a smooth formal
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
-scheme,
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}
, where
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
is a complete non-archimedean DVR with residue field
\mathchar 29035
and fraction field
\mathchar 29003
. The classical tube of
\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29035}
in the generic fiber of
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}
is replaced by the
tempered tube

\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
. We construct this object as a derived analytic space in the ind-Banach setting and show that it is an open analytic subspace of the generic fiber of
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}
, viewed as an ind-Banach derived analytic space. Locally, this open subspace is described by algebras of functions satisfying a logarithmic
growth condition at the boundary.

The tempered cohomology groups
\mathrm{\mathchar 29000}^{\mathchar 29033}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785
are then defined as the (Hodge-completed) derived de Rham cohomology of
\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
. A priori, this construction may depend on the choice of the embedding into
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}
. We prove, however, that in our hypotheses of regularity the resulting cohomology is independent of all choices. For smooth proper
\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29035}
, we show that this cohomology agrees with rigid, equivalently crystalline, cohomology. Thus the theory developed here should be regarded as a tempered analogue of convergent rigid cohomology. Extending it to non-proper varieties will require a tempered counterpart of Monsky-Washnitzer cohomology, involving logarithmic decay conditions at infinity, following ideas proposed by Scholze. We plan to pursue this direction in future work
[
7
]
.

Another possible development concerns the introduction of new open subsets in our ind-Banach derived analytic spaces, defined by logarithmic decay conditions. This would provide a natural geometric framework for the coefficients associated with
\mathchar 29040
-adic representations of the fundamental group. Indeed, generic representations give rise to convergent isocrystals
[
34
]
, while representations with finite local monodromy give rise via this Riemann-Hilbert correspondance to overconvergent isocrystals
[
50
]
.
Between these two classes lie other types of representations, some of which were studied in
[
36
]
and
[
37
]
in connection with logarithmic decay series, but without an underlying geometric framework. We hope that our approach will provide such a framework.

We now describe the article in detail.

3
Ind-Banach modules

Let
\mathchar 29010
be a
non-archimedean Banach ring
. Here we mean that
\mathchar 29010
is a ring equipped with a norm
\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 8705\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29010}
that satisfies the non-archimedean triangle inequality, such that the ring multiplication is bounded, and that
\mathchar 29010
is complete for the metric induced by
\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 8705\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29010}
(see also
[
18
, Section 1.2.1]
). In this section we recall the theory of the category of ind-Banach modules over
\mathchar 29010

[

, Section 3]
and further in
[

,
4
,

]
.

3.1
Quasi-abelian categories of Banach modules

We recall the notion of a non-archimedean Banach module.

Definition 3.1
.

A non-archimedean Banach
module over
\mathchar 29010
is a module over
\mathchar 29010
equipped with a norm
\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 8705\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29005\mathchar 12833{\bf\mathchar 29010}_{\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720}
such that the following conditions hold:

•

\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
, for
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005
, if and only if
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
.

•

\mathchar 29005
is complete for the metric induced by
\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 8705\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}
.

•

\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29037^{\mathchar 560}\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}\mathchar 12820\max\delimiter 67273472\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}\mathchar 24891\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037^{\mathchar 560}\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}\delimiter 84054785
for any
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005
.

•

\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29037\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}\mathchar 12820\left\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29042\right\delimiter 69640972\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037\right\delimiter 0
, for any
\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29010
,
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005
.

We denote by
\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}
the category of non-archimedean
Banach module over
\mathchar 29010
, with morphisms given by bounded
\mathchar 29010
-linear maps. We denote by
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28721}
the wide subcategory of
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}
with the same objects of
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}
and morphism given by
contracting morphisms
, namely those morphisms
\mathchar 29030\mathchar 24634\mathchar 29005\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}
such that

\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}}\mathchar 12820\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}

for every
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005

The following is
[
6
, Proposition 3.18]
.

Proposition 3.2
.

The categories
\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}
and
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28721}
are quasi-abelian.

In fact,
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}
is naturally enriched over abelian groups. The direct sum of two objects
\mathchar 29005
and
\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}
is given by the direct sum of the underlying abelian groups
\mathchar 29005\mathchar 8712\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}
endowed with the norm

\left\delimiter 0\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 8712\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}}\mathchar 12349\max\delimiter 67273472\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29025\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}\mathchar 24891\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29026\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891

for
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005
,
\mathchar 29026\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}
.
If
\mathchar 29030\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29005\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}
is a morphism in
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}
its kernel is given by

Proposition 3.3
.

The category

In particular, given an object

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29008^{\mathchar 560}
be two projective objects in
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}
, then
\mathchar 29008\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 29010}\mathchar 29008^{\mathchar 560}
is also projective.

Proof.

From the discussion above, it follows that every projective is a direct summand of an object of the form
\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29005\delimiter 84054785
, for
\mathchar 29005\mathchar 12850{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}
, it suffices to prove the claim for objects of this form. But if
\mathchar 29005\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12850{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}
, then

\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29005\delimiter 84054785\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 29010}\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\contcoprod_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005\mathchar 8704\{\mathchar 28720\}}\mathchar 29010_{\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}}\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 29010}\contcoprod_{\mathchar 29037^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8704\{\mathchar 28720\}}\mathchar 29010_{\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037^{\mathchar 560}\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}}\mathchar 12839\contcoprod_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005\mathchar 8704\{\mathchar 28720\}}\contcoprod_{\mathchar 29037^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8704\{\mathchar 28720\}}\mathchar 29010_{\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037^{\mathchar 560}\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathchar 314

Here we use that
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28721}
is closed symmetric monoidal, so that the completed tensor product preserves coproducts in each variable (see the proof of
[
6
, Lemma 3.26]
). Applying the same argument as in
[
6
, Lemma 3.26]
, we see that

\contcoprod_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005\mathchar 8814\{\mathchar 28720\}}\contcoprod_{\mathchar 29037^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8814\{\mathchar 28720\}}\mathchar 29010_{\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005}\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29037^{\mathchar 560}\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 560}}}

is projective in
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}
.
∎

By general results on Ind-categories of quasi-abelian categories
[
46
, Proposition 2.1.17]
, the category
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785
is complete and cocomplete and carries a natural elementary quasi-abelian structure. Moreover, the completed tensor product
\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 29010}
extends to a closed symmetric monoidal structure on
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785
(see
[
46
, Proposition 2.1.19]
). In the present Banach setting this is also recalled in
[
6
, Lemma 3.29]
.

Proposition 3.5
.

The category
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785
is a complete and cocomplete closed symmetric monoidal elementary quasi-abelian category with enough projectives. Moreover projective objects of
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785
are flat. Additionally, if
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29008^{\mathchar 560}
are projectives in
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785
, then also
\mathchar 29008\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 29010}\mathchar 29008^{\mathchar 560}
is.

Proof.

The assertions about flatness and stability of projective objects under tensor products follow from the corresponding statements in
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}
, namely from
[
8
, Proposition 3.13]
and Proposition
3.4
, together with
[
46
, Proposition 2.1.19]
.
∎

Assume now that
\mathchar 29010
is a Banach ring over a non-archimedean Banach field
\mathchar 29003
of characteristic
\mathchar 28720
. We will use the following result. We refer to
[
35
, Definition 4.32]
for the definition of a homotopical algebra context.

Theorem 3.6
.

The category
\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
of cochain complexes in
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785
, endowed with the projective model structure, is a homotopical algebra context.

Proof.

This follows from
[
35
, Theorem 4.33]
. Indeed,
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785
is locally presentable by the general properties of ind-completions, it is elementary by
[
46
, Proposition 2.1.17]
, and, by Proposition
3.5
, it has enough projective objects which are flat and closed under tensor products.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

In particular, if
\mathchar 29010
is a Banach ring over
\mathchar 29003
, then the
\mathchar 561
-category
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
is a closed symmetric monoidal stable
\mathchar 561
-category. Its monoidal structure is induced by the left derived functor of
\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 29010}
, which we denote by
\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}
. The same applies to module categories over commutative algebra objects. Namely, if
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12850\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
, then
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785
is again a closed symmetric monoidal stable
\mathchar 561
-category.

We will now give a fundamental example of an object in
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785

3.2
Bornological vector spaces

If
\mathchar 29003
is a non-archimedean non-trivially valued field, the category
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785
is related to the theory of bornological vector spaces over
\mathchar 29003
. We briefly review this concept in this subsection. The main reference for this is
[
6
, Subsection 3.3]
.

Definition 3.9
.

Let
\mathchar 29016
be a set. A
bornology

\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}
on
\mathchar 29016
is a collection of subsets of
\mathchar 29016
that satisfies the following properties:

1.

\mathchar 4947\displaylimits_{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}}\mathchar 28994\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29016
.

2.

If
\mathchar 28994\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}
and
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12826\mathchar 28994
, then
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}
.

3.

If
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}
and
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}
, then
\mathchar 4947\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}
.

A set
\mathchar 29016
equipped with a bornology
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}
is called a
bornological set
. The elements of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}
are called
bounded subsets
.

Definition 3.10
.

A function between two bornological sets
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}\delimiter 84054785
and
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
is said to be
bounded
if it sends bounded subsets for
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}
to bounded subsets for
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28994}^{\mathchar 560}
.

We can now introduce bornological vector spaces over
\mathchar 29003
.

Definition 3.11
.

A bornological vector space over
\mathchar 29003
is a vector space
\mathchar 29014
over
\mathchar 29003
that is also a bornological set, such that the addition and multiplication by scalars are bounded maps.

We denote by
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
the category of bornological vector spaces over
\mathchar 29003
, with morphisms given by bounded
\mathchar 29003
-linear maps.

Remark 3.12
.

There is a fully faithfull embedding

\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}\mathrel{\mathchar 300\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}\mathchar 24891

given endowing each Banach space with the
von Neumann bornology
, for which a subset is bounded if and only if it is absorbed by
all neighborhoods of the origin (see
[
4
, Lemma 3.17]
and
[
32
, p. 102]
). This is the usual notion of boundedness for subsets of Banach spaces.

Proposition 3.13
.

The category
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
is complete and cocomplete.

In particular the forgetful functor
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}\mathchar 12833\mathsf{\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29027}_{\mathchar 29003}
commutes with all limits and colimits.

Definition 3.14
.

A bornological vector space is called
complete
if it is isomorphic to a monomorphic filtered colimit of Banach spaces.

Thus we can define the full subcategory of complete bornological vector spaces
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}\mathchar 12826{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
.
In
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
we have a natural notion of convergence.

Definition 3.15
.

A sequence
\{\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29038}\}_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}
in
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 12850{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}

converges to

\mathchar 28720

in the sense of Mackey
if there exists a bounded subset
\mathchar 28994\mathchar 12826\mathchar 29014
such that for every
\mathchar 28949\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 8706}
there exists an
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}
such that
\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28949\mathchar 28994
for each
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 29006
. A sequence
\{\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29038}\}_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}
in
\mathchar 29014
is said to
converges to

\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29014

in the sense of Mackey
if the sequence
\{\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29025\}_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}
converges to
\mathchar 28720
in the sense of Mackey.

Note that by definition the convergence in the sense of Mackey coincides with the usual convergence in norm for objects in
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
.

Definition 3.16
.

Let
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 12850{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
, a subset
\mathchar 29013\mathchar 12826\mathchar 29014
is called
bornologically closed
if every sequence of elements in
\mathchar 29013
converging in the sense of Mackey converges to an element in
\mathchar 29013
.

The definition above helps to describe the quasi-abelian structure of
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
.

Proposition 3.17
.

The category
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
is quasi-abelian.

Proof.

This is proved in
[
6
, Lemma 3.53]
.
∎

In particular morphism
\mathchar 29030\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28994\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28994^{\mathchar 560}
in
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
is a
strict epimorphism
if it is surjective and
\mathchar 28994^{\mathchar 560}
is endowed with the quotient bornology. Whereas
\mathchar 29030
is a
strict monomorphism
if it is injective,
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994\delimiter 84054785
is bornologically closed in
\mathchar 28994^{\mathchar 560}
, and the bornology on
\mathchar 28994
agrees with the bornology induced by
\mathchar 28994^{\mathchar 560}
.

We now explain the relation between
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}\delimiter 84054785
and
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
.
The following is contained in
[
6
, Proposition 3.60]
.

Theorem 3.18
.

There exists a functor

\mathrm{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12346{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}\mathchar 12833{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}\delimiter 84054785

called
dissection functor
that induces an equivalence of categories between
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
and
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}^{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}\delimiter 84054785
, the category of
essentially monomorphic ind-objects
, and commutes with all limits and all filtered monomorphic colimits. The functor
\mathrm{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29043}
is isomorphic to the identity functor when restricted to
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
. Moreover, the functor is exact in the sense of the theory of quasi-abelian categories.

Theorem
3.18
suggests the following definition.

Definition 3.19
.

Let
\mathchar 29010
be a Banach ring. The category of
complete bornological modules over
\mathchar 29010
is the category of essentially monomorphic objects of
\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 84054785
.

The main reason for introducing the category
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
is the following theorem.

Theorem 3.20
.

The dissection functor induces an equivalence of stable symmetric monoidal
\mathchar 561
-categories

\mathrm{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12346\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 84054785\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathchar 12839}}{{\mathchar 12833}}\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Proof.

This follows from
[
8
, Proposition 3.19]
using the fact that they are stable
\mathchar 561
-categories.
∎

Theorem
3.20
tells us that from the point of view of derived geometry, the categories
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
and
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}\delimiter 84054785
are equivalent. However,
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
is a much more concrete category than
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}\delimiter 84054785
and often permits the application of the methods and theorems of functional analysis. Therefore, we prefer to phrase our results in terms of bornological spaces instead of ind-Banach spaces whenever convenient.

Lemma 4.3
.

The module

is flat because it is a filtered colimit of flat objects in an elementary quasi-abelian category.

We now define multivariable tempered power series.

Remark 4.7

In fact, the hypothesis that

5
The derived analytic spectrum of an ind-Banach ring

Let
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}
be a closed symmetric monoidal
\mathchar 561
-category. In
[
26
, Construction 5.2]
, Clausen and Scholze show that the collection of homotopy epimorphisms from the unit of
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}
(or derived idempotent algebras in the terminology of
[
26
]
) is naturally a distributive lattice. In their construction, this lattice is interpreted as the lattice of closed subsets of a locale, which we denote here by
\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}\delimiter 84054785
. In general this locale need not be spatial, and hence need not come from an ordinary topological space. This point of view is used in
[
26
]
as a foundation for analytic geometry. Classical localizations in the usual models of algebraic and analytic geometry appear as closed subsets in their convention. For instance, if
\mathchar 28993
is a discrete ring and
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29030}
is a localization, then
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29030}
is a homotopy epimorphism in
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785
, and therefore determines a closed subset of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
.
In this section we use the same lattice of homotopy epimorphisms, but with the opposite geometric convention: we construct from it a spectral topological space
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}\delimiter 84054785
whose compact open subsets correspond to homotopy epimorphisms from the unit. Thus
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}\delimiter 84054785
should be regarded as a Hochster-dual version of the Clausen–Scholze spectrum. If
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12850\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
, then

\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\bigl\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785\bigr\delimiter 84054785

will be our model for the derived analytic spectrum of
\mathchar 28993
. This construction is compatible with the classical picture: for a discrete ring
\mathchar 28993
, the localization
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29030}
defines an open subset of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
. Analogous results in analytic settings have been obtained in
[
11
]
,
[
6
]
,
[
4
]
,
[
12
]
,
[
13
]
,
[
9
]
. The subject is broader than this list of references, but these are the works most closely related to the present construction. The space
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}\delimiter 84054785
will be naturally equipped with a structure sheaf valued in
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}
.

5.1
Preliminaries of lattice theory

The main reference for this subsection is
[
33
]
.
Recall that a
lattice
is a partially ordered set
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004\mathchar 24891\mathchar 12820\delimiter 84054785
such that any finite set admits a least upper bound, also called
join
and a greatest lower bound, also called
meet
. In lattice theory, if
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29004
it is common use to denote the join and the meet of
\{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026\}
by
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 8799\mathchar 29026
and
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 8798\mathchar 29026
respectively. More generally if
\mathchar 29011
is a subset of
\mathchar 29004
, the join of
\mathchar 29011
(if it exists) will be denoted by
\mathchar 4951\displaylimits\mathchar 29011
. A lattice
\mathchar 29004
is called
complete
if it admits also all infinite joins.
A lattice is called
distributive
if

\mathchar 29025\mathchar 8798\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\mathchar 8799\mathchar 29027\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025\mathchar 8798\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8799\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025\mathchar 8798\mathchar 29027\delimiter 84054785

for any
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29027\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29004
. We can then define the category
\mathsf{\mathchar 28996\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29044}
of distributive lattices setting its objects to be distributive lattices and morphisms to be functions that preserve finite meets and joins.

Definition 5.1
.

A
frame
is a complete distributive lattice that satisfies the so called
infinite distributive law
:

\mathchar 29025\mathchar 8798\mathchar 4951\displaylimits\mathchar 29011\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4951\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 29025\mathchar 8798\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891

for every
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29004
and for every
\mathchar 29011
subset of
\mathchar 29004
. The category
\mathsf{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29037}
of frames, is the category whose objects are frames and whose morphisms are functions that preserve finite meets and arbitrary joins.

The following is the fundamental example of a frame.

Example 5.2
.

Let
\mathchar 29016
be a topological space. The poset of open subsets of
\mathchar 29016
(where the partial order is given by the inclusion) is a frame. We will denote it
\mathchar 28682\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\delimiter 84054785
.

This example leads naturally to consider the category
\mathsf{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29037}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}}
as the category of a sort of “pointless topological spaces” that we will denote by
\mathsf{\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29027}
. The objects of
\mathsf{\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29027}
are called
locales
.
Let
\mathchar 29004
be a distributive lattice. Recall that a
descending subset

\mathchar 29011
of
\mathchar 29004
is a subset of
\mathchar 29004
such that, if
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011
and
\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29025
, then
\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011
.
An
ideal
of
\mathchar 29004
is a descending subset closed under finite joins.

Example 5.3
.

Let
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29004
, the principal ideal generated by
\mathchar 29025
, denoted by
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025\delimiter 84054785
is the ideal of
\mathchar 29004
whose elements are the
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29004
such that
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29025
.

The set of ideals of
\mathchar 29004
, ordered by inclusion, will be denoted by
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29036}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004\delimiter 84054785
.

Proposition 5.4
.

The poset
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29036}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004\delimiter 84054785
is a frame.
Moreover the functor

\mathchar 29004\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathrm{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29036}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004\delimiter 84054785

defines a left adjoint to the forgetful functor

\mathsf{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 12833\mathsf{\mathchar 28996\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29044}\mathchar 314

Proof.

See
[
33
, Corollary II.2.11]
.
∎

It is easy to see that the meet of two elements
\mathchar 29001\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29001^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12850\mathrm{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29036}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004\delimiter 84054785
is the intersection, whereas the join is the ideal generated by
\mathchar 29001
and
\mathchar 29001^{\mathchar 560}
. This implies the following proposition.

Proposition 5.5
.

The function

\mathchar 29004\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29036}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004\delimiter 84054785

defined by

is a distributive lattice morphism.

Propositions

and

mean that
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29036}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004\delimiter 84054785
is a kind of “frame completion” of
\mathchar 29004
.
We now want to see when we can associate a topological space to a locale.
An ideal
\mathchar 29001

\mathchar 29004

\mathchar 29025

A

\mathchar 29004

\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29004

Let now

The set of prime elements of

If

then we can define the set

\mathchar 28996_{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 29040\mathchar 12850\mathrm{\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29025\ \mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29043}\ \mathrm{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29044}\ \mathrm{\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29028}\ \mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038}\ \mathchar 29040\}\mathchar 314

(6)

A spatial frame is thus canonically associated to a topological space.

Remark 5.10

The functor

\mathchar 29004\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathrm{\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29044\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29036\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785}

Remark 5.14
.

Building on this description of
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 8799\mathchar 28993^{\mathchar 560}
it is easy corollary of Lurie-Barr-Beck theorem that the condition on
\mathchar 28996_{\mathchar 28993}
and
\mathchar 28996_{\mathchar 28993^{\mathchar 560}}
to cover
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}\delimiter 84054785
is equivalent to asking that the family of localizations
\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993^{\mathchar 560}\}
is conservative. In the sense that the restriction functor
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}\mathchar 12833\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8712\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993^{\mathchar 560}}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}\delimiter 84054785
is conservative, where the latter are the categories of
\mathchar 28993
and
\mathchar 28993^{\mathchar 560}
modules in
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}

This is the cover condition of the homotopical Zariski topology introduced in

and used for bornological algebras in

Example 5.18
.

Let
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
be an affinoid algebra over
\mathchar 29003
. Every

The motivation

to construct derived analytic spaces over

\mathchar 28994\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28994\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 28995

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 28995\delimiter 84054785\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28994}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 28995\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28995\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28994}\mathchar 28994\delimiter 84054785\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 28995\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28995\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 28995\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 28994\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28995\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 28994\mathchar 314

Moreover, for two homotopy epimorphisms
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28995
and
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28996

\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28994

because the derived tensor product also commutes with homotopy fibers and direct sums.

(10)

5.3
The affine space over a Banach ring

Let
\mathchar 29010
be a Banach ring. In this subsection we study affine spaces over
\mathchar 29010
in the ind-Banach setting.

Definition 5.20
.

Consider the polynomial algebra
\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12850{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785
introduced in Example
3.8
.
The
\mathchar 29028
-dimensional
affine space
over
\mathchar 29010
is the derived analytic spectrum
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785
. We will denote it by
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29010}
.

The topological space underlying
{\bf\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 29028}
is very large and it is unreasonable to expect a complete description of it. Thus, we limit ourselves to identifying some families of open
subsets of
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29010}
. To do so, we use a criterion used in
[
13
, Section 5]
to identify when a morphism is a homotopy epimorphism. We recall it for the reader’s convenience.

Let
\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28995}\mathchar 24891{\overline{\mathchar 8714}}\delimiter 84054785
be closed symmetric monoidal quasi-abelian category with enough flat projective objects, and let
\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12850\mathsf{\mathchar 28995}
.
Recall that an
element
of
\mathchar 29016
is a morphism
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29016
form the unit object. In the classical category of modules over a ring this notion coincides with the notion of an element of the underlying set. So, we will write
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29016
to say that
\mathchar 29048
is an element of
\mathchar 29016
.

Definition 5.21
.

Let
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12850\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 84054785
,
and let
\mathchar 29025
be an element of

\mathchar 28993
. The
diagonal Koszul resolution
of
\mathchar 28993
, denoted by
\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28993}

\mathchar 28993{\overline{\mathchar 8714}}\mathchar 28993\xrightarrow{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025{\overline{\mathchar 8714}}\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721{\overline{\mathchar 8714}}\mathchar 29025\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 28993{\overline{\mathchar 8714}}\mathchar 28993\mathchar 314

This notation means that the morphism is induced by multiplication by
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025{\overline{\mathchar 8714}}\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721{\overline{\mathchar 8714}}\mathchar 29025\delimiter 84054785
. The pair
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025\delimiter 84054785

the strictness condition
if
\mathchar 28993
is flat and if the morphism

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025{\overline{\mathchar 8714}}\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721{\overline{\mathchar 8714}}\mathchar 29025\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 28993
.

The following example is an example of a pair that satisfies the strictness condition.

Example 5.22
.

The pair
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84054785

{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 84054785

, Lemma 4.6]

The criterion of
[
13
, Section 5]
is expressed in the following theorem.

Theorem 5.23
.

Let
\mathchar 28993\xrightarrow{\mathchar 29030}\mathchar 28993^{\mathchar 560}
be a morphism in

and let
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28993

\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28993^{\mathchar 560}

Proof.

See
[

∎

This criterion is used in

Let
\mathchar 29010\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091

where the second inequality follows from

\mathchar 28722^{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29036\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29036\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12821\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891

for
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}
,
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}
,
\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}
,
\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29043
. For
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
there is nothing to prove, so we assume
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720
. Since all the quantities involved are positive, the inequality is equivalent, after taking the
\mathchar 29038
-th root and then inverting, to

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29036\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29036\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28722\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 314

We prove this latter inequality. Since
\mathchar 28720\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29043
, we have
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29036\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
and

\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29036\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\mathchar 12820\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28722\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29036\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29036\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28722\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 314

Finally, since

, we have

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29036\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29036\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28722\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891

which is equivalent to the desired inequality.
∎

Lemma 5.28
.

The algebra
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 28720}

Remark 5.30
.

The results above give a chain of open subsets of
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}
. More precisely, the natural morphisms of
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779
-algebras

\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 28720}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\lim_{\mathchar 28954\mathchar 12604\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28954^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091

induce inclusions

\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\!\left\delimiter 67273472\lim_{\mathchar 28954\mathchar 12604\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28954^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12826\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12826\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12826\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12826\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 314

We interpret these respectively as the usual open unit disk, the tempered unit disk, the bounded unit disk, and the closed unit disk centered at
\mathchar 28720
(see also

, Theorem 5.8]
).

6
Tempered decomposition of the affine line over a field

In this section we consider the topological space

\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12349\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

We will interpret
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044}}
as the algebra of functions supported on the complement of the tempered disk at infinity, in the sense of Remark
5.16
. We already know that
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044}}
determines an open subset of
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}
by Lemma
5.29
. We will show that this open subset and the punctured tempered disk at infinity cover
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}
, and that their intersection is non-empty. This shows that
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}
can be covered by two open subsets: one associated with the
punctured tempered disk at infinity
, and the other associated with the algebra of fast-converging series at
\mathchar 28720
.

For
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}
and
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29018}
consider

\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 29037}_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\left\{\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29003\mathchar 24891\ \lim_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12833\mathchar 561}{{\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 69640972\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29038}}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28720\right\}\mathchar 314

This is clearly a Banach space over
\mathchar 29003
when endowed with the norm

\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 29037}_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}}\mathchar 12349\max\{\max_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 24891\sup_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}\left\{{{\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 69640972\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29038}}}\right\}\}\mathchar 314

Equivalently, this is the space of Laurent series whose positive part has degree at most
\mathchar 29037
, and whose negative tail has logarithmic growth at most
\mathchar 29038
in the variable
\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
.

We set

\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 29037}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\colim_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}}\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 29037}_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 24891

where the colimit is computed as bornological vector space, and similarly for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}

\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\colim_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}^{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 24891

and finally

\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\colim_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}^{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\colim_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 314

Thus

\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 29037}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\left\{\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29003\mathchar 24891\mathchar 569\ \mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}\ \lim_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12833\mathchar 561}{{\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 69640972\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29038}}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\right\}\mathchar 24891

and

\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\left\{\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29003\mathchar 24891\ \lim_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12833\mathchar 561}{{\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 69640972\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29038}}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\ \text{for some }\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}\right\}\mathchar 314

It is straightforward to check that
\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779
is a bornological algebra over
\mathchar 29003
.
We interpret it as the algebra of functions on the tempered disk at
\mathchar 561
in
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}
. Strictly speaking, the objects defined above live in
{\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}
. Via the dissection functor of Theorem
3.18
, we can regard them as objects of
{\mathsf{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}}^{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472{\mathsf{\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}}\delimiter 84054785
, and we keep the same notation for them.

Notice that
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779
is flat over
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779
or over

. Therefore
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779
is a homotopy epimorphism. Since
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779
is also a homotopy epimorphism, the claim follows by the stability by composition of homotopy epimorphisms.

We call the corresponding open subset

\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12826\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}

the
punctured tempered disk at infinity
. We now compute the algebra associated with the complement of this open subset in
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}
, in the sense of Remark

. We follow the argument of

, Proposition 5.6]
.

Proposition 6.2
.

The algebra of functions supported on the complement of the punctured tempered disk at infinity in
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}
is naturally identified with

, Lecture V]

in
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}
amounts to computing

\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}\underline{\mathrm{\mathchar 29000\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037}}_{\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779}\delimiter 67273472\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\left\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\right\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

(14)

the module of tempered series with negative degrees, shifted by -1. Then (

) is identified to

(15)

We first compute the derived
\mathrm{\mathchar 29000\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037}
s as
\mathchar 29003
-modules.
Notice that
\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
is projective in

(16)

Since the dual of convergent sequences is given by bounded sequences, see
[

, Corollary 3.50]
, we obtain that (

) is isomorphic to

\mathchar 4940\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29036}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 314

To prove the isomorphism

(17)

one takes the cone of the strict (by Corollary

) monomorphism

\mathchar 4940\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29036}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84267779\xrightarrow{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29044\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29045\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 4940\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29036}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 24891

that is
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29038}
.
In fact
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779
is clearly projective over
\mathchar 29003
, and the projective objects over

are direct summands of base changes of projective objects over

by Proposition

So, if
\mathchar 29008^{\mathchar 8719}
is a resolution of
\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84267779
by projective

-modules, we can compute

as

and we obtain (

). We are going to show in the next lemma that the projective system that defines

§3.3, it follows that

concluding the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

The projective limit

\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12833\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}

is nuclear. Therefore,
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044}}
is a nuclear bornological space.

Proof.

It is enough to prove the statement for the cofinal projective system

\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091_{\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29038}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091_{\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 24891

for some constant

∎

Remark 6.4
.

By a similar argument, one can also prove that all the natural maps in the inductive limit defining the tempered functions
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}
are nuclear. Thus,

is a nuclear bornological space as well.

.

,

]
. We plan to investigate these algebras in forthcoming work

]
.

with the tempered disk at

inside the affine line is non-empty.

.

The derived tensor product

Lemma 6.7

Remark 6.8

with

in

. We will discuss this phenomenon further in forthcoming work

]

It remains to check that the family of maps
\{\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044}}\delimiter 84267779\}
is a cover for the homotopy Zariski topology. This is done in the next proposition by a direct computation:

Proposition 6.9
.

There is a Mayer–Vietoris type fiber sequence in
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779}\delimiter 84054785
:

\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 8712\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044}}\mathchar 314

Proof.

The surjective restriction map
\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 8712\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044}}
has kernel
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779
.∎

Remark 6.10
.

In the derived setting, along the previous lines, one can prove (essentially by repeating the computations done in
[
26
, Proposition 5.6]
in the complex analytic case) that the complement of the closed dagger analytic unit disk at infinity is given by the open unit disk at
\mathchar 28720
. In terms of algebras, we get a fiber sequence in
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779}\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003\{\!\{\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\}\!\}\mathchar 8712\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091^{{\mathchar 8825}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29003\{\!\{\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\}\!\}\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091^{{\mathchar 8825}}\mathchar 24891

analogous to that of Proposition
6.9
. Here
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29044\delimiter 86414091^{{\mathchar 8825}}
is the weak completion of the polynomial algebra
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779
i.e. the free dagger algebra, and
\mathchar 29003\{\!\{\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\}\!\}
is the algebra of analytic functions on the open unit disk centered at
\mathchar 561
.

7
Geometric interpretation of log-growth transfer theorems for
\mathchar 29040
-adic differential equations

This section is mainly expository. Its purpose is to explain how a classical transfer phenomenon for
\mathchar 29040
-adic differential equations, traditionally formulated in analytic terms, becomes a simple geometric statement in the derived analytic setting developed above. More precisely, we show that the log-growth transfer theorem can be interpreted as a continuity statement for the spectrum
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785
associated with a suitable affinoid algebra
\mathchar 28993
.

We illustrate this point by revisiting the transfer theorem for solutions of non-archimedean differential equations with logarithmic growth. In general, the term
Transfer Theorem
refers to a result which transfers a property from the “generic point” to a specified point. A classical example concerns the radius of convergence of a
\mathchar 29040
-adic differential equation. In the framework of Berkovich spaces, this phenomenon has been interpreted as a continuity theorem (see
[
2
,
44
]
). There is, however, another kind of transfer theorem, involving not only the radius of convergence at the generic point, but also the logarithmic growth of solutions within their domain of convergence. This type of transfer theorem was introduced by Dwork–Robba
[
28
]
(see also Christol
[
24
, Section 5]
). It was later studied by Chiarellotto–Tsuzuki
[
21
,
22
]
and in subsequent works (see for example
[
51
]
and
[
42
]
).

Our aim is to show that, from the perspective of derived analytic geometry, the Transfer Theorem for logarithmic growth conditions can also be interpreted as a continuity theorem. Indeed, series with logarithmic growth, namely tempered series, can be viewed as analytic functions on a suitable open subset of the affine line. Since continuous maps preserve open subsets under pullback, the transfer theorem for log-growth conditions becomes a natural consequence of continuity.

Proposition 7.2
.

The Amice ring
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}
is isomorphic to the derived tensor product
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 28720}\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 29044}}\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29044\delimiter 84267779
. In particular,

is isomorphic to the derived intersection of two open subsets in
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29003}
.

Proof.

is closed in

8
Tempered tubes: towards a tempered convergent cohomology

We recall that, in our notation,
\mathchar 29035
will be a perfect field that we will see as the residual field of a complete non-archimedean valuation ring,
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
of characteristic 0, whose fraction field will be indicated by
\mathchar 29003
.

In this section we introduce the notion of
tempered tube
of a
\mathchar 29035
-scheme: this should be seen as a log-growth open tube containing the usual tube of the rigid setting (see
[
38
, Chapter 2]
). The aim is to use such a tube to define a tempered analogue of the (rigid) convergent cohomology for
\mathchar 29035
-schemes.

Remark 8.1
.

The guiding example is the scheme given by a point, the origin, of
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29035}
. We may consider the formal scheme
\mathrm{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29030}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29048\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 84054785
, viewed as a lifting of
{\bf\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 28721}
. The generic fiber of
\mathrm{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29030}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29048\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 84054785
is the Tate algebra
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29048\delimiter 86414091
.
In the derived analytic spectrum associated with
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29048\delimiter 86414091
, namely the closed unit disk, the usual tube of the origin is the open unit disk. In the framework developed above, there is also a larger open neighborhood, the tempered tube, whose sections of the structure sheaf are given by the tempered power series
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
.

We now introduce the local construction of the tempered tube. Let
\mathchar 28993
be a topologically finitely generated, flat
\mathchar 29040
-adic formal
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
-algebra, and let
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
denote its associated affinoid
\mathchar 29003
-algebra.

Definition 8.2
.

Let
\mathchar 29016
be an affine algebraic variety over
\mathchar 29035
, and suppose that
\mathchar 29016
is equipped with a closed immersion

\mathchar 29016\mathrel{\mathchar 300\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 24891\qquad{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}\mathchar 12349\operatorname{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29030}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Suppose that the closed immersion
\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12833{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29035}
is defined by the ideal
\delimiter 67273472{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12826\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29035}

\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 68408078\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891

In the next sections we will prove, under suitable hypotheses, that the definition does not depend on the choice of the
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29033}

\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785

\operatorname{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785

[

.

Remark 8.3
.

The definition of the tempered tube is motivated by the analogy with the classical construction. Keeping the same notation, if we denote by
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\{\!\{\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\}\!\}

8.1
The setting
