Lemma 8.4
.

Under the previous hypotheses, the supremum norm of
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
is multiplicative. Moreover, it coincides with the affinoid norm on
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
(see Remark
8.5
).

Proof.

By
[
18
, Proposition 6.2.3.5]
, the supremum norm on
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
is multiplicative if and only if the reduction
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718\mathchar 8718}
is an integral domain, where
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718}
denotes the subring of power-bounded elements and
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718\mathchar 8718}
the ideal of topologically nilpotent elements.
Since
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 8714_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}}\mathchar 29035
is reduced we have that
\mathchar 28993
is integrally closed in
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
by
[
19
, Proposition 1.1]
.
But the power bounded elements
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718}
form precisely the integral closure of
\mathchar 28993
in
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
(see
[
20
, Theorem 3.1.17]
), and so
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28993
since
\mathchar 28993
is smooth (hence normal).
It suffice to show that
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718\mathchar 8718}\mathchar 12349\mathfrak{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 28993
, because
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 8714_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}}\mathchar 29035
is reduced, and thus
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718\mathchar 8718}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28993\mathchar 8714_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}}\mathchar 29035
, that is an integral domain.
The inclusion
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718\mathchar 8718}\mathchar 12827\mathfrak{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 28993
is clear because all element of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 28993
are topologically nilpotent. The other inclusions follows from the fact that if
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8718\mathchar 8718}
and
\mathchar 28953\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 29037}
,
then there exists an
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}
such that
\mathchar 29048^{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28953\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12826\mathfrak{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 28993
, because
\mathchar 29048
is topologically nilpotent. Thus the image of
\mathchar 29048
in
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathfrak{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29035}
is nilpotent. Since

is reduced, this image is zero, and hence
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 28993

∎

From now on we will consider

is discretely valued, repeating this procedure finitely many times gives a representative for which

\max_{\mathchar 29033}\left\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29026^{\mathchar 29033}_{\mathchar 29002}\right\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29002}\mathchar 314

This completes the induction step. Applying the construction to all multidegrees
\mathchar 29002\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}^{\mathchar 29043}
, we obtain a tuple
\mathchar 29000\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29042}\delimiter 84054785
such that

\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}

and

\left\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29032^{\mathchar 29033}_{\mathchar 29002}\right\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29002}

for every coefficient of every
\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}
. Hence

\max_{\mathchar 29033}\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28721\mathchar 314

∎

From the fact that
\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}
is a regular sequence in
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
it easily follows that also the sequence
\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}
is regular in
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779
. Now we want to see that it is a regular sequence in
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
as well.

Lemma 8.7
.

The sequence

\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}

is regular in
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}

Proof.

The sequence

\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}

is regular in
\mathchar 29011_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042}}
. Indeed, after quotienting by
\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
, the element
\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29033}
is still monic in the variable
\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}
, and hence is not a zero divisor. We now prove regularity in
\mathchar 29011_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
. Let
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314
By Lemma
8.6
, the natural map

\mathchar 29011_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29011_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29011_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042}}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29011_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042}}

is injective. Since
\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29033}
is not a zero divisor in the target, it is not a zero divisor in the source.
This holds for every
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29043
, so the sequence is regular in
\mathchar 29011_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
.
∎

Lemma 8.8
.

For every
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}
, the map

\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}^{\mathchar 29043}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\qquad\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}

is strict. In particular, the ideal

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}

is closed in
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathchar 29032
be in the image and suppose
\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29032\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28721
. By Lemma

8.6
, applied with
\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29043
, there exist

\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}
such that

\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}

and

\max_{\mathchar 29033}\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 0_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28721\mathchar 314

This says precisely that the quotient norm on the coimage agrees with the induced norm
on the image. Hence the map is strict. Since
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}
is Banach, the image is closed.
∎

We can now prove the following proposition.

Proposition 8.9
.

The Koszul complex associated with

\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}

is a strict resolution of

{{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 314

Proof.

By Lemma
8.7
, the sequence

\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}

is regular in
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
. In particular, its restriction to each Banach level
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}
is algebraically exact in the sense needed here: the image of each Koszul differential is the kernel of the next one. Thus the only point is strictness. For the last differential

\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}^{\mathchar 29043}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\qquad\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\mapstochar{{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}}}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891

strictness follows from Lemma
8.8
. For the previous differentials, their images are kernels of continuous maps, hence are closed Banach subspaces. By the open mapping theorem, every continuous surjection from a Banach space onto a closed image is strict. Therefore all Koszul differentials are strict epimorphisms onto their images, and the Koszul complex is strictly exact on each Banach level
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}
. Passing to the filtered colimit over
\mathchar 29038
gives the strict resolution in
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
.
∎

As a by-product of the lifting lemma, we obtain the following comparison between the algebra associated with the tempered tube and the algebra associated with the usual rigid analytic tube.

Corollary 8.11
.

There is a canonical injective morphism of
\mathchar 29003
-algebras

{{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785}}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}{{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\{\!\{\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\}\!\}\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 314

Proof.

is injective. This is a consequence of the equality

Proposition 8.12
.

The canonical morphism

\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}{{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785}}

Proof.

{{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720

this is clear. So we assume the contrary.

{{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785}}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}{{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785}}\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}}\dots\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}}{{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29043}}\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 314

(22)

In fact by Lemma

\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 68408078\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785
admits the flat resolution (see Lemma

)

\mathchar 28720\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\xrightarrow{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28720\mathchar 314

And so
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\widehat{\mathchar 8714}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}}\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 68408078\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785

.
Thus (

) follows by induction.
It suffices then to prove the lemma for
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
. Consider the homotopical pushout diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 86414091}

) we have that
\mathchar 28967
is a homotopy epimorphism. We can conclude using the fact that the Weierstrass localization

is a homotopy epimorphism (see

and the lemmas before it) and the stability of homotopy epimorphisms by composition.
∎

We can finally prove that, in our setting, the tempered tube is independent of the chosen presentation of the regular immersion.

Proposition 8.17
.

The tempered tube
\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
is independent of the choice of regular sequence defining
\mathchar 29016
in
{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29035}
and of the chosen liftings.

Proof.

Suppose that the ideal of
\mathchar 29016
in

\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29043}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 314

with

. Choosing liftings

, we obtain

By the same argument in the other direction, using expressions of the
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 29034}
in terms of the
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29033}
, we obtain a morphism in the opposite direction. These two morphisms are inverse to each other. Indeed, after composing with the canonical injections into the corresponding algebras of functions on the rigid tube, constructed in Corollary

Our aim is now proving a tempered version of the so called “Weak fibration theorem”, see
[

, Corollary 2.3.16]
for the rigid setting. We need the following preliminary result.

Lemma 8.18
.

. For
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721
, set

\mathchar 29002_{\mathchar 29006}\mathchar 12349\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049^{\mathchar 28939}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4945\displaylimits\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28939\mathchar 12850{{\bf\mathchar 29006}}^{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28939\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 12821\mathchar 29006\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28953^{\mathchar 29006}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12826\mathchar 28993\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 314

By base change, the morphism

\mathchar 28993\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29002_{\mathchar 29006}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28993^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29002_{\mathchar 29006}

is étale. Modulo the nilpotent ideal generated by

\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 24891\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28953\mathchar 24891

it is an isomorphism, because
\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29016
. Since an étale morphism which is an isomorphism modulo a nilpotent ideal is itself an isomorphism, the preceding map is an isomorphism for every
\mathchar 29006
.
Taking the limit over all
\mathchar 29006
we have that

\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785

is an isomorphism. By tensoring by
\mathchar 29003
and via base change to
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
we get the isomorphism
\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
.
∎

We can now prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 8.19

(Tempered weak fibration theorem)
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}^{\mathchar 560}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 29016}

be a smooth morphism of regular formal embeddings (in the sense of

, Chapter 2]
) of
\mathchar 29016
into affine
\mathchar 29040

Then locally on
\mathchar 29016
the morphism

induces an isomorphism

\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 8706_{\mathchar 29003}\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Proof.

By

{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}
and
{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}^{\mathchar 560}
are affine and
\mathchar 29045

followed by the projection
\widehat{\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}_{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}^{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12833{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}

, we can replace

with

to embed

\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 12839\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{\widehat{\mathbf{\mathchar 28993}_{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}^{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 68408078\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 8704\widetilde{\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12839\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 314

9
The analytic cotangent complex and the tempered convergent rigid cohomology

In this section we will globalize the definition of the tempered tube. We will use this to associate with a scheme
\mathchar 29016
over
\mathchar 29035
a well-defined tempered convergent cohomology which is independent of the choices made to define the tempered tube (under some hypotheses of regularity on the embedding of
\mathchar 29016
in a smooth formal scheme).
Such a cohomology will be the “tempered” version of the classical convergent rigid cohomology: the tube of radius one (strictly less than 1) of
\mathchar 29016
on which one calculates the de Rham cohomology will be replaced by the tempered tube. For this reason we will introduce the ind-Banach version of the Kähler differentials and de Rham cohomology, that are the analytic cotangent complex and derived de Rham cohomology.

9.1
Derived analytic stacks and spaces

Let
{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}
be a
\mathchar 29040
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
, and let
{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29003}
be its generic fiber.
Consider an affine open cover,
\{\mathrm{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29030}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}
, of
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}
, then the affinoid spaces
\mathrm{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785
form an admissible open affinoid cover of
{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29003}
. We can associate with
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}
the derived analytic spectrum
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785
, equipped with its structure sheaf, we introduced so far (see Definition
5.17
).

In order to globalize this construction we use the functor-of-points perspective. We look at global derived analytic spaces as sheaves over the (formal) homotopical Zariski site

\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12349\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29039}\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 314

More precisely, we endow
\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}
with the homotopical Zariski topology: open immersions are induced by homotopy epimorphisms of bornological algebras, and covers are finite families of such morphisms whose pullback functors on module categories form a conservative family.
We call
\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}
the category of
affine derived analytic spaces
over
\mathchar 29003
. It is not difficult to check that the spectrum functor introduced so far
\mathchar 28993\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785
realizes the category
\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}
as a subcategory of the category of Ind-Banach homotopically-ringed topological spaces.

Definition 9.1
.

A
derived analytic stack
over
\mathchar 29003
is a
\mathchar 561
-functor

\mathscr{\mathchar 28998}\mathchar 12346\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 12833\mathsf{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 24891

where
\mathsf{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29043}
is the
\mathchar 561
-category of spaces, such that
\mathscr{\mathchar 28998}
satisfies
\mathchar 561
-descent for the Zariski covers introduced so far. We denote by
\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29035}_{\mathsf{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}
the full
\mathchar 561
-subcategory of
\mathsf{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 24891\mathsf{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785
determined by derived analytic stacks.

Remark 9.2
.

By Definition
9.1
, a derived analytic stack is a sheaf for the homotopical Zariski topology. We do not impose hyperdescent. Thus our notion is the ordinary sheaf condition in the sense of
[
39
, Chapter 7]
, rather than the stronger condition of being a hypercomplete sheaf. These two notions need not coincide in general, even on coherent sites (see
[
39
, Counterexample 6.5.4.5]
).

Remark 9.3
.

It is standard computation to check that the homotopy Zariski topology is subcanonical.

Thus, by Remark
9.3
, the Yoneda embedding
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12346\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12833\mathsf{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 24891\mathsf{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785
factors through
\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29035}_{\mathsf{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}
. Moreover, for any
\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12850\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}
we obtain a small site over
\mathchar 29016
by restricting the Zariski topology given by the opens induced by homotopy epimorphism from
\mathchar 28993
, if
\mathchar 29016
is in duality with
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12850\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
. The small Zariski site associated to
\mathchar 29016
is equivalent to the site associated with the topological space
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785
introduced so far. We therefore think to
\mathchar 29016
as being represented by the spectrum
\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785
.

Definition 9.4
.

A
derived analytic space
over
\mathchar 29003
is an object of
\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29035}_{\mathsf{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}
that can be written as a colimit of a diagram of representable objects whose morphisms are open Zariski immersions. In such a situation we say that the derived analytic space is obtained by glueing the affine analytic spaces.

Remark 9.5
.

Definition
9.4
can be reformulated by saying that
\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12850\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29035}_{\mathsf{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}
is a derived analytic space if it admits an affine Zariski atlas. This means that there exists an effective epimorphism

\mathchar 4960\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 29013_{\mathchar 29033}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891

where each
\mathchar 29013_{\mathchar 29033}
is an affine derived analytic space and each morphism
\mathchar 29013_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29016
is a Zariski open immersion, and for every
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034
, the fiber product
\mathchar 29013_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8706_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 29013_{\mathchar 29034}

is again a affine derived analytic space.

In particular, in the situation where
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}
is a
\mathchar 29040
-adic formal scheme and
\{\mathrm{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29030}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}
a formal affine cover of it, we get a diagram
\{\mathrm{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 8714_{\mathchar 29008}\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785\}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}
of affinoid spaces and open affinoid embeddings. Passing to generic fibers, this gives a diagram of affine derived analytic spaces

\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28720}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785

via the C̆ech nerve of the cover. We define the analytification of
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}
to be the colimit of this C̆ech diagram in
\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29035}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}

\widehat{\mathchar 29008}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 12349\colim_{\mathchar 28673}\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28720}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

This construction is independent of the chosen affine covering. As shown in the next remark:

Remark 9.6
.

Let

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 24634\mathsf{\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040}_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}

be the generic fiber functor from affine

-adic formal schemes of topologically finite type over

to affine derived analytic spaces over
\mathchar 29003
. It sends

,
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785
. Precomposition with
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29003}
induces an adjunction

\mathchar 29004\mathchar 12346\mathsf{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040}_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 24891\mathsf{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785\leftrightarrows\mathsf{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 24891\mathsf{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785

the left adjoint,
\mathchar 29004
, is the left Kan extension via
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29003}
. Since
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29003}
is continuous (it follows from the main results of

that it sends covers for the Zariski topology of formal schemes to covers for the homotopical Zariski topology), the adjunction restricts to an adjunction

\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29032\delimiter 69640972}_{\mathsf{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040}_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}}\delimiter 84054785\leftrightarrows\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29035}_{\mathsf{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 314

Thus, if

as a sheaf in
\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29032\delimiter 69640972}_{\mathsf{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040}_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785
then

\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\widehat{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\colim_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\colim_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\widehat{\mathchar 29008}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 314

This shows that the association
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\widehat{\mathchar 29008}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 29038}
is functorial and therefore, given any other cover
\{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29034}\}
of

we get that computing the analytification
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 29038}
using this cover yields the same result, up to equivalence.

Remark 9.7
.

By the same reasoning, any rigid analytic space over
\mathchar 29003
can be viewed as an object of
\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29035}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}
, by writing it as a Zariski gluing of affinoid spaces and applying the functor-of-points construction above.

We now recall the definition of quasi-coherent sheaves on prestacks. On affine derived analytic spaces we have the assignment

\operatorname{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29027}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

This assignment extends to arbitrary prestacks by right Kan extension along the Yoneda embedding. Thus, for a prestack

\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12850\mathsf{\mathchar 28998\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 24891\mathsf{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891

we define

\mathsf{\mathchar 29009\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29032}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 12349\lim_{\operatorname{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29027}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29016}\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891

where the limit is taken over the category of affine derived analytic spaces mapping to
\mathchar 29016
.

We now check that if
\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12850\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29035}_{\mathsf{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}
is a derived analytic space obtained glueing the family of affine spaces
\{\mathchar 29013_{\mathchar 29033}\}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}
then we can compute

\mathsf{\mathchar 29009\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29032}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\lim_{\mathchar 28673}\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28720}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29040}}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Proposition 9.8
.

The functor
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785
is a sheaf of
\mathchar 561
-categories on
\mathsf{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29030}_{\mathchar 29003}

Proof.

The same argument in
[

, Proposition 10.5]
applies here. Namely, for a homotopical Zariski cover
\{\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29033}\}
, the category
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785
is recovered as the limit of the corresponding C̆ech nerve of the diagram of module categories. The key points are that the morphisms
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29033}
are homotopy epimorphisms and that the associated family of pullback functors on module categories is conservative.
∎

Let us now introduce sheaves on
\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29035}_{\mathsf{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}

supposing it small in a bigger, tacitly fixed, universe.
, following

Proposition 9.11
.

Let
\mathchar 29016
be a derived analytic space obtained by gluing affine derived analytic spaces
\mathchar 29013_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785
. Let

\mathchar 29013_{\mathchar 8719}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29016

be the C̆ech nerve of this affine cover, and write
\mathchar 29013_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28720}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 12349\mathfrak{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28720}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 84054785
. Then

\mathsf{\mathchar 29009\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29032}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 12839\lim_{\mathchar 28673}\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28720}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29040}}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Proof.

We have seen that
\mathsf{\mathchar 29009\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29032}_{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\delimiter 84054785}
is a sheaf on
\mathsf{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29035}_{\mathsf{\mathchar 29018\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29003}
and hence commutes with all limits.
∎

Thus
\mathsf{\mathchar 29009\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29032}_{\mathchar 29016}
can be computed by the derived C̆ech complex associated with any affine Zariski cover of
\mathchar 29016
.

Remark 9.12
.

The limit of symmetric monoidal
\mathchar 561
-categories by a system of strictly monoidal functors has a canonical structure of symmetric monoidal
\mathchar 561
-category (see
[
31
, Chapter 3, §3.2.1–3.2.2]
). In particular this is true for
\mathsf{\mathchar 29009\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29032}_{\mathchar 29016}
and we will denote its monoidal structure by
\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 29016}
. This makes it possible to apply the spectrum construction of Section
5.2
to
\mathsf{\mathchar 29009\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29032}_{\mathchar 29016}
. On the other hand, if
\mathchar 29016
is a derived analytic space, one can also construct a homotopically ringed space by gluing the spectra of the affine pieces in an affine cover. Under suitable hypotheses, these two constructions should agree. This suggests an analytic analogue of the Balmer–Rosenberg reconstruction theorem.

9.2
The analytic cotangent complex

Our aim now is to define the
cotangent complex
of the derived analytic space
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 29038}
associated with the generic fiber of a formal
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
-scheme,
{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}
. We will use it in order to define derived analytic de Rham cohomology. Our definition of cotangent complex is taken from that one given in
[
49
]
.

The definition of the cotangent complex is local for the homotopy Zariski topology. We may therefore begin with the affine case. Let
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}\mathchar 12349\operatorname{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29030}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785
be an affine
\mathchar 29040
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
, with
\mathchar 28993
a topologically finitely generated
\mathchar 29040
-adic
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
-algebra, and let
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28993\mathchar 8714_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}}\mathchar 29003
denote its analytic generic fiber. If
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}
is obtained as the
\mathchar 29040
-adic completion of an affine
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
-scheme
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 12349\operatorname{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29027}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994\delimiter 84054785
, then we also write
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28994\mathchar 8714_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}}\mathchar 29003
for the algebraic generic fiber. In this situation there is a natural morphism
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
, which is a sort of analytification morphism.

We will use the classical algebraic cotangent complex, whose definition we now recall. We denote by
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29031}\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29031}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}}\delimiter 84054785
the closed symmetric monoidal
\mathchar 561
-categories of algebraic modules over
\mathchar 29003
and
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
respectively, with their standard monoidal structures given by
\mathchar 8714_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}
and

\mathchar 8714_{{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}}}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}
. These can be presented via suitable model categories of chain complexes and satisfy the hypothesis of
[
49
]
. In this setting, following
[
49
, page 27]
, the (absolute)
cotangent complex

{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}
is the object of
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29031}\delimiter 84054785
defined by

{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}\mathchar 12349{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}{\mathrm{\mathchar 29009}}{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}{\mathrm{\mathchar 29001}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 8714^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Here the functor
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001}
is defined in the following way. It takes a commutative algebra
\mathchar 28995
over
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
in
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785
equipped with a morphism

to

. This means that we have the following diagram of maps

\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\xrightarrow{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28995\xrightarrow{\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 24891

and then
\mathchar 29001

-algebra. The second functor

\mathchar 28995\mathchar 8714_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}}\mathchar 28995\xrightarrow{\mathchar 28950}\mathchar 28995

and

\mathchar 28682^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}

In
[

, page 58]
, it is proven that, in the algebraic setting this definition coincides with the classical Illusie-Quillen one. The free
\mathchar 29003
-polynomial resolutions are cofibrant in the underlying model category. In particular in the case where

{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28682^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}\mathchar 24891

By considering the analogue construction as in the algebraic case, we have

where the completed tensor product takes the place of the algebraic tensor product and the objects are considered as ind-Banach modules.

Remark 9.13
.

It is clear from the definition of the cotangent complex that if
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28995
is a homotopy epimorphism, then
\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28995\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}}}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28720
.

Now we would like to compare
{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}

{\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}}_{{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}

\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}

\mathchar 29003
-algebra. In order to formulate this comparison inside the category of bornological vector spaces, we need to recall the fine bornology. This bornology was already used implicitly in Example

Definition 9.14
.

Let
\mathchar 29014
be a
\mathchar 29003
-vector space. Let us write
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4940\displaylimits_{\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28994}\mathchar 29003

on

in
\mathsf{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 28994\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29003}

Notice that Definition
9.14
is well-posed because
\mathchar 29003
has only one bornology, up to isomorphism, that is separated and compatible with its Banach structure.

Example 9.15
.

The most basic object of
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785

The fine bornology defines a functor
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29029}}\mathchar 12346\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29031}\mathchar 12833\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29003}

. In particular, it sends algebraic commutative differential graded

-algebras to commutative differential graded algebras in bornological
\mathchar 29003
-vector spaces. When no confusion can arise, we will omit the functor

We will use the following results from

Proposition 9.16

Theorem 9.17
.

Assume that
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
is a finitely generated smooth
\mathchar 29003
-algebra and that

{\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}}_{{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}{\widehat{\mathchar 8714}}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}}{\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}{\widehat{\mathchar 8714}}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}}{\mathchar 28682^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 314

Proof.

By Proposition
9.16
, the map
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
is a homotopy epimorphism. Therefore, we can localize the cotangent complex applying

because the relative cotangent complex of
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
vanishes. This gives the first isomorphism.

We remark that for
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
we have

\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29029}}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28682_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 28721}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}{\widehat{\mathchar 28682}}_{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29029}}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 28721}

as
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
-modules, because we suppose
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
smooth and the functor
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29029}}
is strongly exact and strongly monoidal, therefore it sends cofibrant
\mathchar 29003
-algebras (i.e. polynomial algebras) to cofibrant bornological
\mathchar 29003
-algebras (i.e. polynomial algebra with the fine bornology). We conclude from the fact that
{\mathchar 28682^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078{\mathchar 29003}}}
is locally free and finite rank as
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
-module and hence flat for the functor
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}}\mathchar 8704
. Moreover the sheaf of differentials on the rigid analytification of the affine scheme
\operatorname{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29027}\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
, is a coherent sheaf (it is the analytification of the coherent module of the algebraic differential, see
[
1
]
). The affinoid algebra
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
identifies ad an admissible affinoid open in such a space, hence the last isomorphism.
∎

Remark 9.18
.

The same argument applies in the relative case. Namely, for a morphism of affinoid completions arising from a morphism of smooth algebraic
\mathchar 29003
-algebras, the relative analytic cotangent complex is obtained by derived completed base change from the corresponding algebraic relative cotangent complex.

All these constructions are local for the homotopy Zariski topology, and therefore can be glued and we can associate a quasi-coherent sheaf of modules which we will indicate by
\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12850\mathsf{\mathchar 29009\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29032}_{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 29038}}
on the derived analytic space
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 29038}
. The object
\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}
is well defined because by Remark
9.5
, derived analytic spaces are
\mathchar 28720
-geometric stacks and, by general results like
[
49
, Proposition 1.4.1.8 and Proposition 1.4.1.11]
, one can check that such geometric stacks have a well-defined cotangent complex. Alternatively, one can check that the compatibility of the cotangent complex with base change through homotopy epimorphisms give a well-defined object of
\mathsf{\mathchar 29009\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29032}_{\mathchar 29016}
.

Remark 9.19
.

This construction can be applied also to the various exterior powers of
\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}

\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}
: they still are quasi-coherent sheaves of modules over
{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29003}^{\mathrm{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29038}}
.

More explicitly,

\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}^{\mathchar 8719}_{\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12839\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\operatorname\mathchar 8707{\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29037}_{\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}\operatorname{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29044}^{\mathchar 8712}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29006}\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}^{\mathchar 8235\mathchar 8719}_{\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}_{\mathchar 8704\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8719}
it the

\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\mathchar 28682_{\mathchar 8704\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8719}

The complex
\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}_{\mathchar 8704\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8719}
differs in general from the Hodge-completed analytic derived de Rham complex, which is obtained by taking the direct product realization of the double complex. Indeed, the
Hodge completed analytic derived de Rham complex
is defined

\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}^{\mathchar 8798\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8719}_{\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12349\operatorname{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29044}^{\scalebox{0.55}{$\mathchar 4945\displaylimits$}}\bigl\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}^{\mathchar 8235\mathchar 8719}_{\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\bigr\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}\lim_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850{\bf\mathchar 29006}}\operatorname{\mathchar 29012\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29044}^{\scalebox{0.55}{$\mathchar 4945\displaylimits$}}\bigl\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}^{\mathchar 8235\mathchar 8719}_{\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28998^{\mathchar 29006}\bigr\delimiter 84054785

where
\mathchar 28998^{\mathchar 29006}
denotes the Hodge filtration.
In the smooth and finite dimensional situation considered in this paper, however, the cotangent complex is a finite projective module of finite rank. Its exterior powers therefore vanish in sufficiently high degrees, and the direct sum and Hodge-completed realizations coincide.

Our goal (Theorem
9.22
) is to compute
\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}_{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8719}
for
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}
a smooth affinoid
\mathchar 29003
-algebra which is the generic fiber of the
\mathchar 29040
-adic completion of a finitely generated smooth
\mathchar 29003
-algebra
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
.

Proposition 9.20
.

Let
\mathchar 29003\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
be a smooth finitely generated
\mathchar 29003
-algebra. Then there is a canonical quasi-isomorphism

\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\mathchar 28682_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8719}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28682_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8719}\mathchar 314

After equipping
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
and its modules of differentials with the fine bornology, one also has

\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}^{\mathchar 8719}_{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29029}}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12839\bigl\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28682^{\mathchar 8719}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\bigr\delimiter 84054785_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29029}}\mathchar 314

Proof.

Since
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
is smooth over
\mathchar 29003
, its cotangent complex is concentrated in degree zero and the canonical morphism
\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28682^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}
is an equivalence. Moreover,
\mathchar 28682^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}
is a finite projective
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}
-module. It follows that

\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\mathchar 28675^{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12839\mathchar 28675^{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28682^{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28682^{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 314

Under these identifications, the
\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 8235}
-differential induced by the universal derivation is precisely the usual de Rham differential. The corresponding statement in the bornological setting follows from the fact that the fine bornology functor is exact and strongly symmetric monoidal.
∎

As the cotangent complex, the analytic derived de Rham complex is compatible with extension of scalars along homotopy epimorphisms.

Proposition 9.21
.

Let
\mathchar 28994\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993
be a homotopy epimorphism of commutative algebras in
\mathcal{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 561}\delimiter 67273472\mathsf{\mathchar 29005\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 8235}
-differential graded commutative algebras

\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}^{\mathchar 8235\mathchar 8719}_{\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12839\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}^{\mathchar 8235\mathchar 8719}_{\mathchar 28994\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 28994}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}\mathchar 28993\mathchar 314

Proof.

Since
\mathchar 28994\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993
is a homotopy epimorphism, we have that
\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12839\mathchar 28993\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 28994}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28994\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}
, by
[
49
, Proposition 1.2.1.6]
. Also, derived exterior powers commute with extension of scalars, and hence, for every
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720

\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\mathchar 28675^{\mathchar 29038}\bigl\delimiter 67273472\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\bigr\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28993\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\mathchar 28994}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\mathchar 28675^{\mathchar 29038}\bigl\delimiter 67273472\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}_{\mathchar 28994\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}\bigr\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

These equivalences are compatible with the
\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 8235}
-differentials, giving the equivalence.
∎

Proposition
9.21
in particular applies to the case of the homotopy epimorphism
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29003}

Theorem 9.22
.

Let

\mathchar 29003
-algebra, endowed with the fine bornology, and let

In particular, the analytic derived de Rham complex of

agrees with its usual analytic de Rham complex of bounded differentials.

Proof.

By Proposition

, the morphism

Corollary 9.23

\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\widehat{\mathchar 28682}_{\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8719}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}\mathchar 28682_{\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8719}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29029}}\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29029}}}^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29004}}\mathchar 28993\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28682_{\mathchar 28994\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29003}^{\mathchar 8719}\widehat{\mathchar 8714}_{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29030\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29029}}}\mathchar 28993\mathchar 314

9.3
Tempered convergent rigid cohomology

We now have all the ingredients to define the tempered convergent rigid cohomology. Throughout this section,
\mathchar 29016
will be a variety over
\mathchar 29035
, and assume that we are given a regular closed immersion
\mathchar 29016\mathrel{\mathchar 300\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29035}
, where
\widehat{\mathchar 29008}
is a formal
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
-scheme, smooth and separated. Under these hypotheses, we define the tempered tube
\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29016\delimiter 67482370_{{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
by gluing the affine tempered tubes introduced above (see Definition
8.2
).
Indeed, let
{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29033}
be an affine open cover of
{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}
in which the closed immersions
\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29016\mathchar 8796{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathrel{\mathchar 300\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}
are defined by a regular sequence (such a cover always exists by
[
48
,
Lemma 0E9J
]
).
We have that

The next theorem is one of our main results.

.
