Lemma 9.28

(Tempered Poincaré lemma)
.

The complex

\mathchar 28720\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993\mathrel{\mathchar 300\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\xrightarrow{{{\mathrm{\mathchar 29028}\over\mathrm{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 29048}}}\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28720

is strictly exact, where
\mathchar 28993
is any bornological
\mathchar 29003
-algebra.

Proof.

Define the evaluation map

\operatorname{\mathchar 29029\delimiter 69640972}_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 24634\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28993\mathchar 24891\qquad\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 29048^{\mathchar 29038}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 24891

and the formal integration operator

\mathchar 29032\mathchar 24634\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\,\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29048\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\mathchar 24891\qquad\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 29048^{\mathchar 29038}\,\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29048\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720}{{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29038}\over\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29048^{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 314

We check that
\mathchar 29032
is bounded. Since
\mathchar 29003
has characteristic zero and carries a non-archimedean valuation, there exist constants
\mathchar 28995\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 29027\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720
such that

\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29038\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29038^{\mathchar 29027}

for every integer
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721
. Thus division by
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
increases the growth of the coefficients by at most a polynomial factor. Consequently, for every Banach level
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29042}
of
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
, the operator
\mathchar 29032
induces a bounded map

\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29042}\,\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29048\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027}\mathchar 314

It therefore defines a bounded morphism on the filtered colimit
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\!\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779\!\delimiter 84267779_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}
. Hence the complex is split exact in the bornological sense, and therefore strictly exact.
∎

We conclude with a comparison theorem relating our tempered cohomology to classical
\mathchar 29040
-adic cohomology theories. In particular, this will imply the finite dimensionality of tempered cohomology for smooth proper varieties.

Proposition 9.29
.

Let
\mathchar 29016
be a smooth proper
\mathchar 29035
-scheme admitting a regular closed immersion into a smooth
\mathchar 29040
-adic formal
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
-scheme of the type considered above. Then there are natural isomorphisms

\mathrm{\mathchar 29000}^{\mathchar 29033}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathrm{\mathchar 29000}^{\mathchar 29033}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29049\mathchar 29043}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8714_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}}\mathchar 29003\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathrm{\mathchar 29000}^{\mathchar 29033}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29031}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

If moreover
\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29035}
is the special fiber of a smooth and proper
\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}
-scheme
\mathchar 29016_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29014}}
, then

\mathrm{\mathchar 29000}^{\mathchar 29033}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29040}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathrm{\mathchar 29000}^{\mathchar 29033}_{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29003}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Proof.

By Berthelot’s comparison theorem
[
15
]
, we know that crystalline and rigid cohomologies of
\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29035}
coincide. The same method of proof will work to get an isomorphism with tempered cohomology.
In fact, we can argue locally for the Zariski topology for all the aforementioned cohomology theories. As in Theorem
9.27
can reduce to an affine covering
\{\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29033}\mathrel{\mathchar 300\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}{\widehat{\mathchar 29008}}_{\mathchar 29033}\}
and compare the complexes that compute the cohomology groups on these affine open subspaces. Then, we all such complexes for the crystalline, (convergent) rigid and tempered cohomologies, are independent upon the smooth closed embedding. But because
\mathchar 29016_{\mathchar 29033}
is smooth affine a smooth lifting can be built and it will give a closed smooth embedding. In this case the various tubes used to define the cohomology theories (the divided powers tube, the rigid one and tempered tube) are all defined by reduction on the maximal ideal. Hence, they all coincide, and this gives the first claim of statement.
The comparison between the crystalline, hence for the tempered, and de Rham cohomology of the generic fiber is classical from
[
14
]
(if the
\mathchar 29035
-scheme is proper and smooth).
∎
